Lines that pretend to be the tool settings, a switch, a run or an audit event are unusable lines, counted, and nothing is handed on; reading a backup writes nothing.

**What was checked.** `tools/verify-13-6.mjs`, 39 checks under plain Node (run through `guarded.mjs`; it removes nothing): A the estimate (what a program printed counts as the next message; the call with ten long arguments counts about 700 tokens; five full runs add 30,000 to 40,000 tokens and the default question is not asked, a small amount is), B the tool is not a search and its output is cut, C the sources of the loop (nothing new: the same rule as for files), D the tool settings and the log sealed both ways, with the key lost, and a hostile line, E backups (the notes, nothing of the switch or the log, seven kinds of damaged note, hostile lines, the restore code names nothing of programs). **12 negative controls** (`make-specs136.mjs`), all noticed: the slice changes no product code, so they take out the existing rules the checks lean on (the record of the turn in the estimate, the result as the next message, the note reader's title, end, exit code, time, counts and keys, the log reader's end and time, and the two kept-document entries in the wiring); the first draft of one of them left a dangling `if` and was a syntax error, which the suite noticed by crashing: it was rewritten.

**Not checked (13.6).** A real provider's count of the tokens of a run's output (the guess is one token to three bytes); a backup restored into an app whose run switch is on (the switch is not in the file, so nothing happens to it).

Status: 13.6 DONE (2026-10-10).

## D-044, addendum of 10 October 2026: window runs and the regression run from a disposable copy of the tree (`tools/guarded-window.mjs`, `tools/guarded-window-copy.mjs`)

**The guard for window runs** (commit `71a68a5`, `tools/guarded-window.mjs`, `tools/harness-guard-preload.mjs`, `tools/guarded-window-selftest.mjs`; `verify-fsguard.mjs` 109 checks, 11 more controls, all noticed except one equivalent mutant that is named in the suite). The owner's second permission of 9 October (after the incident) allowed a window run and the regression to remove only (a) the throwaway profile folders `frontier-app-test-scratch-<name>` directly inside the Roaming folder, the ones a check reuses before a launch and never the real profile `frontier-app`, and (b) the generated `out\` folder of the copy; nothing on A:, nothing else. `guarded-window.mjs` makes that true in code for every Node process of a run: `NODE_OPTIONS=--import=harness-guard-preload.mjs` installs the guard of `fsguard.mjs` in each (writes are not limited; removals are refused outside the run's own folder, the pattern of the throwaway profiles and the repo's `out`). What it cannot watch is Electron's own processes (they ignore `--import`) and programs a check starts.

**What the first real use found (13.5).** The first window run of Stage 13.5 through that guard, from the working tree, did not start: the dev tool the window checks use (electron-vite, vite, npm) removes files of its own while it works: a temporary config file next to the project's config (`electron.vite.config.<number>.mjs`, made and removed at every start), vite's cache folder `node_modules\.vite\deps`, and npm's old log files in its cache folder. The guard refused all three (so nothing was removed, and the dev server said so in words and exited). Those removals are not in the owner's two permissions, and widening the permission is not mine to do, so the run was changed rather than the permission: **`tools/guarded-window-copy.mjs` runs the script from a disposable COPY of the tree made INSIDE the run's own folder** (`C:\frontier-tmp\guarded-w-<random>\app`: the repo without `.git`, `out`, `tools\.out` and the plan PDFs, and a real copy of `node_modules`, made by `robocopy`, which copies and removes nothing). Everything the tools remove is then something the run itself made inside `C:\frontier-tmp`, which is what the owner's first permission allows; the outputs of the run (`tools\.out`) are copied to `C:\frontier-work\window-runs\<run>\` before the run's folder is removed; npm's cache and logs are the run's own (`npm_config_cache`). Every sabotage of a negative control is made in the COPY: **a window run never changes the working tree.** The wrapper refuses to start when `node_modules` is a junction (a junction would let the dev server write on the drive it leads to): the working copy on C: has a real `node_modules` now (the junction to A: is parked at `C:\frontier-work\node_modules-junction-to-A`, renamed, never removed).

**Left behind by the first attempt (not removed, by the rule):** one file, `electron.vite.config.<number>.mjs`, in the root of the working copy on C: (the tool's own temporary file, whose removal was refused); it is not committed.

**The folder of runs in a window run.** The app makes `FrontierRun` at the ROOT of the workspace's drive (13.3); a window run must not make or remove anything at the root of C:. Putting the workspace on a substituted drive that is an alias for a folder of the run was tried first and the app REFUSED it, on purpose ("That folder is reached through a link, a substituted drive or a short name", 12.1): the right answer. `programBox.ts` now takes an optional fixed folder of runs, which `ipc.ts` fills from the environment variable `FRONTIER_RUN_ROOT` **only when the app is not installed** (`!app.isPackaged`, the rule of `FRONTIER_API_BASE_URL`, `baseUrl.ts`) and which is ignored unless it is an ordinary absolute folder below a drive (never a drive's root, never a network path); `tools/run135.mjs` names a folder inside the run's own folder. Checked in `verify-13-5.mjs` (part G, 8 checks) with 4 controls, all noticed (the sabotaged ones were stopped by the file guard before they could make anything at a drive root, which is what it is for).

**Found while running the regression (10 October 2026): the throwaway profiles were being written to drive A:, and why.** The first full regression through this wrapper failed at every suite that removes a key or a profile in the throwaway profile: the guard refused `remove of ...\Roaming\frontier-app-test-scratch-s5reg\credentials.anthropic.json`, a file inside a folder it had just allowed. The reason is not in the guard but in Windows: the Claude desktop app is a packaged (MSIX) app installed on drive A:, and **Windows redirects everything its processes write under the real AppData (Roaming and Local) to the package's own data folder on A:** (`A:\WpSystem\S-1-5-21-...\AppData\Local\Packages\Claude_pzs8sxrjxfjjc\LocalCache\...`). Every test process the app starts inherits that. So, for every stage so far, **the throwaway profiles `frontier-app-test-scratch-*` of the window runs (94 folders, 1.26 GB now) have physically been on drive A:**, inside the app's own package folder, and so were the old stores of the EFS checks; the "EFS-encrypted AppData" of this PC (D-010) is that folder (it is the package's store that is encrypted; plain folders on C: are not, `cipher` says "The request is not supported"). I had told the owner I was keeping off A: after the incident; **this redirect was not something I knew, and it has been writing there all the time**, as the Claude app itself does. Three things were changed because of it:

1. The guard no longer judges a removal in a throwaway profile by the real path of the file (which is the redirected one, with no link anywhere) but looks name by name for a link or junction on the way (`linkFree` in `fsguard.mjs`): a link is still no way out (what is behind a junction in a profile is refused; the junction itself may go), checked in `verify-fsguard.mjs` part H and by two controls (`gPatternReal`, `gPatternLinkLast`).
2. **Window runs now have their own AppData** (`guarded-window-copy.mjs`: `APPDATA` and `LOCALAPPDATA` are `<run>\roaming` and `<run>\local`, on C:, in the run's folder): their throwaway profiles, Chromium's caches and the app's key files are made and removed there, never on A:. The checks of the EFS conditions of this PC cannot be reproduced there (EFS is not available on C:); the few older plain-Node suites that test them (the store suites of 4.1, 5.3, 5.4, 7.4, 8.1 to 8.3, 8b.1 to 8b.3, 9.1) keep the real `APPDATA` (`FRONTIER_REAL_APPDATA`), which is the redirected one: they write a few small files there and remove them, as they always did.
3. Nothing already there was removed: the 94 old profiles are still in the package folder on A: (1.26 GB, mine, from all stages); only the owner can say whether they may go.
